## run_pipeline.py

Macro-driver forecasting pipeline - entry point.

### `main`

```python
main()
```

Command line entry: parse arguments, load the config, start the trace and run the pipeline.

**Config:** `config_file`, `run_name`

**Calls:** `run`, `load_config`, `refresh`, `preflight`, `finish`, `start`

<sub>run_pipeline.py:55</sub>

### `run`

```python
run(cfg, args)
```

The full pipeline: data -> features -> both selection approaches -> model zoo -> backtest, diagnostics and scenarios for every model -> tables, charts and summary.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `cfg` |  | configuration. |
| `args` |  | parsed command-line arguments. |

**Config:** `approaches`, `cache.dir`, `cache.enabled`, `data.bank.target_column`, `en_first.stability.threshold`, `features.deflator`, `models.horizon_combination`, `out_dir`, `run_name`, `sample.horizon`, `sample.report_horizons`, `save`

**Calls:** `all_features`, `candidate_forms`, `evaluate`, `horizon_summary`, `buckets`, `by_horizon`, `combine`, `describe`, `final_choice`, `scenarios`, `build`, `selection_info`, `wanted`, `has_formula`, `write`, `best_per_family`, `chart_family_bars`, `chart_horizon`, `chart_regimes`, `chart_scenarios`, `chart_stability`, `scenario_models`, `summary_md`, `write_tables`, `build`, `StepCache`, `StepCache.write_manifest`, `hash_code`, `hash_frame`, `hash_obj`, `Context`, `log`, `save_all`, `snapshot`, `add_story`

**Called by:** `main`

<sub>run_pipeline.py:79</sub>

### `transform`

```python
transform(mdf, code, tr, deflator=None)
```

Apply one stationary transform to one macro series.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `mdf` |  | macro DataFrame (quarters x macro mnemonics). |
| `code` |  | mnemonic of the series, e.g. "FHPI.ION". |
| `tr` |  | transform code: diff, diff2, diff4, lvl, rgap, dlog, yoy, dlog2, acc, gap, rdlog (real growth). |
| `deflator` | `None` | mnemonic of the price index, used by the real transforms. |

**Returns:** Series on the same quarterly index (NaN where the transform needs more history).

**Calls:** `window`

**Called by:** `candidate_forms`, `feature_series`, `build`, `main`

## src/modelling/ardl.py

ARDL model used as the final model by both approaches:

### `ols_np`

```python
ols_np(X, y)
```

Plain OLS with numpy (fast; used inside the exhaustive search).

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `X` |  | regressor matrix (n x k), including the constant. |
| `y` |  | target vector (n). |

**Returns:** (coefficients, standard errors, residuals). Standard errors are classical (not Newey-West).

**Called by:** `exhaustive_search`, `channels_data_measures`, `core_plus_addons`, `main`

<sub>src/modelling/ardl.py:15</sub>

### `vif`

```python
vif(F)
```

Variance inflation factors of the columns of F (multicollinearity check).

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `F` |  | matrix of drivers (n x k). |

**Returns:** array of k VIFs (1 when k < 2, inf if the correlation matrix is singular).

**Called by:** `diagnostics`, `exhaustive_search`, `core_plus_addons`, `search`, `main`

<sub>src/modelling/ardl.py:31</sub>

### `make_panel`

```python
make_panel(ctx, mdf, features)
```

Quarterly panel for one scenario: target, dummies and features (history + scenario path).

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `ctx` |  | the Context: loaded data, organic growth, scenarios, metadata and the estimation window. |
| `mdf` |  | macro DataFrame (quarters x macro mnemonics): the history or one scenario. |
| `features` |  | driver feature names ("code\|transform\|Llag"). |

**Config:** `features.deflator`

**Calls:** `feature_matrix`

**Called by:** `ARDL.panel`, `AutoARDL.panel`, `LevelsARDL.panel`, `ARIMAX.panel`, `DeepModel.panel`, `Theta.panel`, `ECM.panel`, `FoundationModel.panel`, `MLModel.panel`, `_Regime.panel`, `SameGrowth.panel`, `main`

<sub>src/modelling/ardl.py:48</sub>

### `ARDL`

```python
ARDL(name, features, ar=True, family='', note='')
```

Every model type (ARDL, ECM, machine learning, combinations, foundation models) has the same interface: panel(ctx, mdf) builds its data, fit(P, start, end) estimates on [start, end], forecast(P, origin, H) returns H quarterly log changes, and spec() describes it for the cache.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `name` |  | display name. |
| `features` |  | driver feature names. |
| `ar` | `True` | include lagged target growth. |
| `family` | `''` | model family for the comparison. |
| `note` | `''` | free text shown in the model list. |

**Called by:** `build`, `rebuild`, `models_at`, `main`

<sub>src/modelling/ardl.py:59</sub>

```python
__init__(name, features, ar=True, family='', note='')
```

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|

<sub>src/modelling/ardl.py:98</sub>

### `ARDL._X`

```python
_X(P)
```

Regressor matrix: constant, lagged growth, drivers and dummies.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `P` |  | panel from panel(). |

**Returns:** DataFrame of regressors on P's index.

**Called by:** `ARDL.fit`

<sub>src/modelling/ardl.py:106</sub>

### `ARDL.fit`

```python
fit(P, start, end, fast=False)
```

fast=True: plain least squares, no standard errors (used inside combinations' weight estimation).

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `P` |  | the model's data panel (target y, drivers, D_ dummies), from model.panel(). |
| `start` |  | first quarter of the estimation window (Period). |
| `end` |  | last quarter of the estimation window (Period). |
| `fast` | `False` | plain least squares without standard errors. |

**Calls:** `ARDL._X`

<sub>src/modelling/ardl.py:124</sub>

### `ARDL.forecast`

```python
forecast(P, origin, H)
```

Part of the model interface (see src/models/zoo.py): iterate the estimated equation forward from an origin.

Lagged growth is replaced by the model's own forecast after the first quarter; drivers come from P (actual values in the backtest, the scenario path in scenario forecasts); dummies are zero.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `P` |  | panel. |
| `origin` |  | last known quarter (Period). |
| `H` |  | quarters ahead. |

**Returns:** array of up to H forecasts of quarterly log growth.

<sub>src/modelling/ardl.py:142</sub>


## src/modelling/evaluate.py

Shared evaluation of a final model (any type): rolling backtest, diagnostics, driver table, scenario forecasts.

### `backtest`

```python
backtest(ctx, model, P, paths_out=None, models_out=None)
```

Re-estimate at every origin from backtest_start, forecast 1..H quarters with realised macro values, and compare the cumulated balance with the organic actual balance.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `ctx` |  | Context. |
| `model` |  | any model. |
| `P` |  | its panel. |
| `paths_out` | `None` | optional list; the quarter-by-quarter forecast paths (a DataFrame) are appended to it. |
| `models_out` | `None` | optional dict; the model as estimated at each re-estimation origin is stored in it (origin label -> pickle bytes), so every fitted version can be reloaded later. |

**Returns:** (errors by horizon, coefficients at each re-estimation).

**Config:** `sample.estimation_start`, `sample.horizon`

**Calls:** `freeze`, `test_rows`, `backtest_origins`, `covid_mask`, `refit_flags`, `error`

**Called by:** `evaluate`

<sub>src/modelling/evaluate.py:13</sub>

### `test_rows`

```python
test_rows(ctx, model, o, fc, act, err, refit=True)
```

The forecast path from one origin, quarter by quarter, next to what happened.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `ctx` |  | Context. |
| `model` |  | the model. |
| `o` |  | position of the origin. |
| `fc` |  | forecast log growth. |
| `act` |  | actual organic log growth. |
| `err` |  | balance error (fraction). |
| `refit` | `True` | whether the model was re-estimated at this origin. |

**Returns:** DataFrame: model, family, origin, date, horizon_q, forecast and actual growth (% q/q), balance at the origin, forecast and actual balance, error (%), whether the model was re-estimated at this origin.

**Calls:** `growth_display`, `path`

**Called by:** `backtest`

<sub>src/modelling/evaluate.py:68</sub>

### `horizon_summary`

```python
horizon_summary(by_h, horizons, col='MAPE_exCOVID_pct', bench='AR(1) benchmark')
```

One row per model: average error over 1..h and error at exactly h, for each h in `horizons`.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `by_h` |  | errors by model and horizon (backtest output). |
| `horizons` |  | comparison horizons, e.g. [4, 8, 12]. |
| `col` | `'MAPE_exCOVID_pct'` | error column to summarise. |
| `bench` | `'AR(1) benchmark'` | benchmark model for the improvement columns. |

**Called by:** `run`

<sub>src/modelling/evaluate.py:92</sub>

### `diagnostics`

```python
diagnostics(ctx, model, P)
```

Residual tests of a model estimated on the full sample.

Ljung-Box autocorrelation (4 and 8 lags), CUSUM stability, Breusch-Pagan, Jarque-Bera, maximum VIF and sign checks. Non-regression models get a row saying the tests do not apply.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `ctx` |  | Context. |
| `model` |  | any model. |
| `P` |  | the model's panel. |

**Returns:** dict, one row of comparison/diagnostics.

**Config:** `sample.estimation_start`

**Calls:** `vif`, `_expected_sign`, `AutoARDL.long_run`

**Called by:** `evaluate`

<sub>src/modelling/evaluate.py:117</sub>

### `driver_sensitivity`

```python
driver_sensitivity(ctx, model, P, H=8)
```

How much each driver's scenario path moves the forecast: baseline path against the driver held at its last actual value, all else equal. Works for every model type (regressions, trees, networks).

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `ctx` |  | Context. |
| `model` |  | a fitted model. |
| `P` |  | its panel (history + base scenario). |
| `H` | `8` | quarters ahead. |

**Returns:** dict feature -> (effect on growth in pp per year, effect on the balance after H quarters in %).

**Calls:** `error`, `per_year`

**Called by:** `driver_table`

<sub>src/modelling/evaluate.py:173</sub>

### `driver_table`

```python
driver_table(ctx, model, P=None)
```

How each driver enters the model, for the driver tables.

ARDL: coefficient, Newey-West t-statistic, expected sign and long-run multiplier; ML: importance or standardised coefficient; combinations: member weights.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `ctx` |  | Context. |
| `model` |  | an estimated model. |
| `P` | `None` | the model's data panel (target y, drivers, D_ dummies), from model.panel(). |

**Returns:** DataFrame, one row per term.

**Calls:** `_describe_term`, `_driver_rows`, `driver_sensitivity`

**Called by:** `evaluate`

<sub>src/modelling/evaluate.py:216</sub>

### `fitted_values`

```python
fitted_values(ctx, model, P)
```

In-sample one-step-ahead fit of a model estimated on all history, for every quarter of the estimation window.

Uses only the model interface (forecast one quarter from each quarter t-1 with the actual data), so it works for every model type; for a regression it equals X·b.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `ctx` |  | Context. |
| `model` |  | model already estimated on all history. |
| `P` |  | its panel. |

**Returns:** DataFrame: model, family, date, actual, fitted, residual (the target's change in the model's form) and the level implied by the fitted change from the previous actual level; None if the model cannot do it.

**Calls:** `level_after`

**Called by:** `evaluate`

## src/modelling/search.py

Shared final step: try every combination of min_vars..max_vars candidates, keep those that satisfy the
economic and statistical rules, rank them on out-of-sample balance error, and pick the simplest model
within `selection_tolerance` of the best.

### `backtest_origins`

```python
backtest_origins(ctx)
```

Forecast origins: from sample.backtest_start to sample.backtest_end (default: the last quarter that still has an actual to compare with), every sample.backtest_step quarters.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `ctx` |  | the Context: loaded data, organic growth, scenarios, metadata and the estimation window. |

**Config:** `sample.backtest_end`, `sample.backtest_start`, `sample.backtest_step`

**Called by:** `backtest`, `exhaustive_search`, `core_plus_addons`, `backtest`, `quick_error`, `_model_steps`, `sur_backtest`, `vecm_backtest`, `run`, `bvarx_backtest`, `joint_backtest`

<sub>src/modelling/search.py:13</sub>

### `refit_flags`

```python
refit_flags(ctx, origins, every=None)
```

True where the coefficients are re-estimated. With sample.reestimate_every = 4 and reestimate_quarter = 1 the model is estimated once a year, at the first origin in Q1-Q4 of each year counted from Q1; at the other origins the same coefficients are kept and only the forecast is redone from the latest actual data. With reestimate_every = 1 (default) every origin re-estimates.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `ctx` |  | the Context: loaded data, organic growth, scenarios, metadata and the estimation window. |
| `origins` |  | positions of the forecast origins in ctx.idx. |
| `every` | `None` | quarters between re-estimations (None = sample.reestimate_every). |

**Config:** `sample.reestimate_every`, `sample.reestimate_quarter`

**Calls:** `BankFeatures.array`

**Called by:** `backtest`, `exhaustive_search`, `core_plus_addons`, `backtest`, `quick_error`, `_model_steps`, `sur_backtest`, `vecm_backtest`, `run`, `bvarx_backtest`, `joint_backtest`

<sub>src/modelling/search.py:23</sub>

### `covid_mask`

```python
covid_mask(ctx, origins, H)
```

Mark backtest errors whose forecast window touches the COVID dummy quarters.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `ctx` |  | Context. |
| `origins` |  | positions of the forecast origins. |
| `H` |  | horizon. |

**Returns:** bool array (origins x H); True = left out of the error metrics.

**Config:** `sample.dummy_quarters`

**Called by:** `backtest`, `exhaustive_search`, `core_plus_addons`, `_model_steps`

<sub>src/modelling/search.py:43</sub>

### `fast_backtest`

```python
fast_backtest(F, y, D, est0, origins, H, refit=None)
```

Level % errors of iterated ARDL forecasts. F: features (T x k), y: target, D: dummies (T x d). refit: boolean per origin (see refit_flags); False keeps the previous coefficients.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `F` |  | candidate features (DataFrame, one column per feature). |
| `y` |  | target vector. |
| `D` |  | dummies matrix. |
| `est0` |  | position of the first estimation quarter. |
| `origins` |  | positions of the forecast origins in ctx.idx. |
| `H` |  | forecast horizon in quarters. |
| `refit` | `None` | bool per origin (None = every origin). |

**Calls:** `error`

**Called by:** `exhaustive_search`, `core_plus_addons`

F: candidate features on the history index; info: DataFrame indexed by feature with theme, expected_sign.

## src/models/autoardl.py

Auto-ARDL models, as in EViews' ARDL estimator: the lag lengths are chosen by an information criterion.

### `_ic`

```python
_ic(ssr, n, k, ic)
```

Information criterion of an OLS fit (EViews' per-observation form; only the ranking matters).

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `ssr` |  | sum of squared residuals. |
| `n` |  | observations. |
| `k` |  | estimated coefficients. |
| `ic` |  | "aic", "bic" or "hq". |

**Called by:** `select_orders`

<sub>src/models/autoardl.py:34</sub>

### `select_orders`

```python
select_orders(y, X, fixed, max_p, max_q, ic='bic', min_p=1, max_models=20000)
```

Choose the lag of y and of every regressor together by an information criterion, EViews style.

All candidate models are estimated on the same sample (the one the longest lags allow), so their criteria are comparable.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `y` |  | Series, the dependent variable. |
| `X` |  | DataFrame of regressors (lag 0). |
| `fixed` |  | DataFrame always included (dummies) or None. |
| `max_p` |  | most lags of y. |
| `max_q` |  | most lags of each regressor (0 = same quarter only). |
| `ic` | `'bic'` | "aic", "bic" or "hq". |
| `min_p` | `1` | fewest lags of y. |
| `max_models` | `20000` | cap on the grid (max_q is lowered to fit). |

**Returns:** dict with p, q (dict regressor -> lag), ic value, models tried.

**Calls:** `_ic`

**Called by:** `AutoARDL.fit`, `LevelsARDL.fit`

<sub>src/models/autoardl.py:45</sub>

### `AutoARDL`

```python
AutoARDL(name, base, max_p=4, max_q=4, ic='bic', family=FAMILY, note='', signs=None)
```

ARDL in growth form whose lags are chosen by AIC/BIC at every estimation (EViews' automatic selection).

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `name` |  | display name. |
| `base` |  | driver features; their lag is ignored (lags 0..max_q are candidates). |
| `max_p` | `4` | most lags of target growth. |
| `max_q` | `4` | most lags of each driver. |
| `ic` | `'bic'` | "aic", "bic" or "hq". |
| `family` | `FAMILY` | as for ARDL. |
| `note` | `''` | as for ARDL. |
| `signs` | `None` | expected sign per base variable "code\|transform" (optional). |

**Called by:** `build`, `rebuild`

| `signs` | `None` | expected sign per base variable "code\|transform" (optional). |


<sub>src/models/autoardl.py:309</sub>

### `uecm_design`

```python
uecm_design(W, lr_names, p, q)
```

The conditional error-correction form of ARDL(p, q): the same model as the levels ARDL, rewritten.

dy*_t = c + pi_y y*_{t-1} + sum_j pi_j z_{j,t-1} + sum_{i=1..p-1} phi_i dy*_{t-i} + sum_j sum_{k=0..max(q_j-1,0)} theta_jk dz_{j,t-k} + dummies

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `W` |  | panel window (with __ys, the long-run columns and the dummies). |
| `lr_names` |  | long-run columns. |
| `p` |  | lag orders of the levels ARDL. |
| `q` |  | lag orders of the levels ARDL. |

**Returns:** (dependent series, regressor DataFrame) with complete rows only.

**Called by:** `bounds_test`

<sub>src/models/autoardl.py:349</sub>

### `bounds_test`

```python
bounds_test(W, lr_names, p, q)
```

Pesaran, Shin & Smith (2001) bounds test for a long-run relation, case III (unrestricted constant, no trend), as in EViews: F-test that all lagged levels are zero, and t-test on lagged y*, in the conditional error-correction form of the chosen ARDL (dummies included).

Critical values: F from PSS Table CI(iii) (statsmodels' copy, by number of regressors k), with asymptotic p-values; t from PSS Table CII(iii).

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `W` |  | estimation window of the panel. |
| `lr_names` |  | long-run columns. |
| `p` |  | chosen lags. |
| `q` |  | chosen lags. |

**Returns:** dict: F, t, the 10/5/1% I(0) and I(1) bounds of each, F p-values, decision, k, n_obs.

**Calls:** `uecm_design`

**Called by:** `LevelsARDL.fit`

<sub>src/models/autoardl.py:377</sub>

```python
base_of(feature)
```

'FHPI.ION|dlog|L1' -> 'FHPI.ION|dlog'.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `feature` |  |  |

**Calls:** `parse_feature`

**Called by:** `build`

## src/models/combination.py

Forecast combinations: an average of member models' forecasts.

### `Combination`

```python
Combination(name, members, weighting='equal', family='Hybrid', note='', window=16, hmax=4, shrink=0.5)
```

Forecast combination: a weighted average of member models' forecasts.

weighting "equal" gives equal weights; "inv_mse" gives Bates-Granger weights from each member's recent out-of-sample errors (last `window` origins, horizons 1..hmax), shrunk by `shrink` towards equal weights.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `name` |  | display name. |
| `members` |  | list of models. |
| `weighting` | `'equal'` | "equal" or "inv_mse". |
| `family` | `'Hybrid'` | as for ARDL. |
| `note` | `''` | as for ARDL. |
| `window` | `16` | origins used for the weights. |
| `hmax` | `4` | horizons used. |
| `shrink` | `0.5` | share of the way back to equal weights (0 = pure inverse-MSE). |

**Called by:** `build`

<sub>src/models/combination.py:11</sub>

### `Combination.__init__`


Deep-learning and classic statistical time-series challengers.


Check whether darts and PyTorch are installed.

**Called by:** `build`

```python
_dummies(P)
```

Names of the COVID dummy columns in a panel.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `P` |  | panel. |

**Returns:** list of str.

**Called by:** `ARIMAX._exog`, `DeepModel._cov_cols`, `Theta.detail_rows`, `Theta.forecast`

<sub>src/models/deep.py:48</sub>

### `DeepModel`

```python
DeepModel(name, kind, features, settings=None, note='')
```

Small neural forecaster from darts (TiDE, N-HiTS, TFT, N-BEATS or TSMixer) trained on this data.

The target and drivers are standardised with training-window statistics; several random seeds are averaged; the 12-quarter path is built from 4-quarter blocks. A fit is skipped when the training data are unchanged.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `name` |  | display name. |
| `kind` |  | tide, nhits, tft, nbeats or tsmixer. |
| `features` |  | driver features (covariates). |
| `settings` | `None` | epochs, input_chunk, output_chunk, n_models, batch_size, learning_rate, refit_every, and per-model overrides such as {"tft": {"epochs": 50}}. |
| `note` | `''` | free text. |

**Called by:** `build`

## src/models/ecm.py

Error-correction model (Engle-Granger two-step), in the spirit of Hofmann (2001) and Calza, Gartner & Sousa (2001).

### `long_run_column`

```python
long_run_column(mdf, item, deflator)
```

One long-run regressor. form: log | log_real (deflated by CPI) | level | real_rate (rate minus y/y inflation).

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `mdf` |  | macro DataFrame (quarters x macro mnemonics): the history or one scenario. |
| `item` |  | {mnemonic, form} with form log, log_real, level or real_rate. |
| `deflator` |  | mnemonic of the price index. |

**Called by:** `LevelsARDL.panel`, `ECM.panel`

<sub>src/models/ecm.py:18</sub>

### `ECM`

```python
ECM(name, short_run, long_run, deflator, ar=True, family='', note='')
```

Error-correction model (Engle-Granger two-step).

Step 1 estimates the long-run relation of the real target level with its long-run drivers; step 2 is an ARDL for target growth that adds last quarter's deviation from that relation (the error-correction term).

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `name` |  | display name. |
| `short_run` |  | short-run driver features. |
| `long_run` |  | list of {mnemonic, form} items. |
| `deflator` |  | price index mnemonic used to make the target level real. |
| `ar` | `True` | as for ARDL. |
| `family` | `''` | as for ARDL. |
| `note` | `''` | as for ARDL. |

**Called by:** `build`, `rebuild`

<sub>src/models/ecm.py:37</sub>

### `ECM.__init__`

```python
__init__(name, short_run, long_run, deflator, ar=True, family='', note='')
```

Create an ECM.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `name` |  | display name. |
| `short_run` |  | short-run driver features. |
| `long_run` |  | list of {mnemonic, form} items. |
| `deflator` |  | price index mnemonic used to make the target level real. |
| `ar` | `True` | as for ARDL. |
| `family` | `''` | as for ARDL. |
| `note` | `''` | as for ARDL. |


## src/models/horizon.py

Horizon combinations: a different model for each forecast horizon.

### `buckets`

```python
buckets(cfg, H)
```

The horizon buckets, clipped to the horizon and extended to cover every quarter up to it.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `cfg` |  | configuration (models.horizon_combination.buckets). |
| `H` |  | forecast horizon. |

**Returns:** list of (first, last) quarter pairs.

**Config:** `models`

**Called by:** `run`, `combine`, `final_choice`

<sub>src/models/horizon.py:31</sub>

### `_bucket`

```python
_bucket(h, B)
```

Index of the bucket that holds horizon h (the last bucket for horizons beyond it).

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `h` |  |  |
| `B` |  |  |

**Called by:** `_splice`, `scenarios`

<sub>src/models/horizon.py:56</sub>

### `_sq_err`

```python
_sq_err(P, cfg)
```

Squared error used to rank models within a bucket (see the module notes).

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `P` |  | test rows. |
| `cfg` |  | configuration (models.horizon_combination.criterion: growth \| balance). |

**Returns:** Series.

**Config:** `models`

**Called by:** `combine`, `final_choice`

<sub>src/models/horizon.py:64</sub>

### `_choose`

```python
_choose(hist, models, B, top_k, min_hist)
```

Best and weighted choice per bucket from realised forecast errors.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `hist` |  | realised test rows (model, origin, horizon_q, sq_err). |
| `models` |  | candidates available now. |
| `B` |  | buckets. |
| `top_k` |  | models in the weighted version. |
| `min_hist` |  | origins needed. |

**Returns:** list per bucket of dict(best=name, weights={name: w}, fallback=bool, mse=float).

**Called by:** `combine`, `final_choice`

<sub>src/models/horizon.py:79</sub>

### `_splice`

```python
_splice(cur, choice, B, how)
```

One origin's combined growth path.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `cur` |  | test rows of every model at this origin. |
| `choice` |  | _choose() result. |
| `B` |  | buckets. |
| `how` |  | best \| weighted. |

**Returns:** DataFrame (one row per horizon) with the combined growth and the actual growth, or None.

**Calls:** `_bucket`, `error`, `from_display`, `path`

**Called by:** `combine`

<sub>src/models/horizon.py:110</sub>

### `combine`

```python
combine(paths, cfg, H, wanted=lambda name: True)
```

Horizon combinations on a set of test forecast paths (backtest or out of sample).

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `paths` |  | one row per model, origin and quarter ahead (columns model, family, origin, date, horizon_q, forecast_growth_pct, actual_growth_pct, balance_at_origin, actual_balance_organic, covid_window). |
| `cfg` |  | configuration. |
| `H` |  | horizon. |
| `wanted` | `lambda name: True` | filter on the combination names (models.only). |

**Returns:** (combined paths in the same format plus error, choices per origin and bucket); (None, None) when switched off.

**Config:** `models`

**Calls:** `_choose`, `_splice`, `_sq_err`, `buckets`, `wanted`

**Called by:** `run`, `run`

<sub>src/models/horizon.py:144</sub>

### `by_horizon`

```python
by_horizon(rows)
```

Errors by quarters ahead, in the format of the backtest table (balance error, COVID windows left out).

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `rows` |  | combined paths from combine(). |

**Returns:** DataFrame: model, family, horizon_q, MAPE_all_pct, MAPE_exCOVID_pct, bias_exCOVID_pct, n_origins, re_estimations.

**Called by:** `run`

<sub>src/models/horizon.py:185</sub>

### `final_choice`

```python
final_choice(paths, cfg, H)
```

The choice for the real forecast: every realised test error counts.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `paths` |  | test forecast paths. |
| `cfg` |  | configuration. |
| `H` |  | horizon. |

**Returns:** (buckets, choice per bucket) or (buckets, None).

**Config:** `models`

**Calls:** `_choose`, `_sq_err`, `buckets`

**Called by:** `run`

<sub>src/models/horizon.py:204</sub>

### `scenarios`

```python
scenarios(scen, B, choice, wanted=lambda name: True)
```

Scenario forecasts of the horizon combinations, spliced from the chosen models' scenario paths.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `scen` |  | scenario forecasts of every model (model, family, scenario, date, dlog_forecast, balance_forecast). |
| `B` |  | buckets. |
| `choice` |  | final_choice() result. |
| `wanted` | `lambda name: True` | filter on the names. |

**Returns:** DataFrame in the same format (empty if nothing to splice).

**Calls:** `_bucket`, `wanted`, `level_after`, `path`

**Called by:** `run`

### `describe`

```python
describe(B, choice, kind)
```

Model-list text and driver-table rows for a horizon combination.

|---|---|---|

## src/models/literature.py

The literature workbook (config: literature.workbook, e.g. config/literature_workbook.xlsx) and its concept table.

### `_yes`

```python
_yes(v)
```

True for yes/true/1/y (any case), as used in the include and core columns.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `v` |  | cell value. |

**Returns:** bool.

**Called by:** `read_workbook`

<sub>src/models/literature.py:24</sub>

### `_settings_value`

```python
_settings_value(v)
```

A settings cell as text ('' for empty).

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `v` |  | cell value. |

**Returns:** str.

**Called by:** `read_workbook`

<sub>src/models/literature.py:56</sub>

### `read_workbook`

```python
read_workbook(path, full=False)
```

Read the literature workbook.

Sheets: "models" (one row per driver of each published model, with its expected sign), "concepts" (the literature table), and optionally "settings" (error-correction model, channels, regime threshold, sign rule) and "references".

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `path` |  | .xlsx file. |
| `full` | `False` | also return the settings and references. |

**Returns:** (models, concepts) or, with full=True, a dict with models, concepts, settings (dict) and references.

**Calls:** `_settings_value`, `_yes`, `legacy_columns`

**Called by:** `load_config`, `check`, `main`, `propose`

<sub>src/models/literature.py:68</sub>

### `clean_settings`

```python
clean_settings(settings)
```

Tidy the settings sheet: text values, and Excel's TRUE/FALSE or yes/no read the way each setting means them.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `settings` |  | dict setting -> cell value (from read_workbook(full=True)); may hold booleans, numbers or NaN. |

**Returns:** dict setting -> cleaned value: apply_signs_to_metadata / ecm_enabled as bool; channels as "table", "off" or a list of concept ids; ecm_long_run as "table" or a list of "name:form"; ecm_short_run as "core" or a list; regime_threshold as "variable|transform|Llag" or "" (= the default); names and sources as text.

**Called by:** `apply_settings`, `check`

<sub>src/models/literature.py:133</sub>

```python
apply_settings(cfg, settings)
```

Put the workbook's settings into the configuration (literature.ecm, channels, sign rule, regime threshold).

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `cfg` |  | configuration (changed in place). |
| `settings` |  | dict from read_workbook(full=True). |

**Config:** `literature.apply_signs_to_metadata`, `literature.channels`, `literature.ecm`

**Calls:** `clean_settings`

**Called by:** `load_config`

## src/models/ml.py

Machine-learning challengers: ridge, random forest, gradient boosting and a small neural network.

### `make_estimator`

```python
make_estimator(kind, params)
```

Build the scikit-learn estimator for a machine-learning model.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `kind` |  | ridge, random_forest, gradient_boosting or neural_net. |
| `params` |  | overrides of DEFAULTS[kind]. |

**Returns:** unfitted estimator (the neural net is an averaged ensemble of small MLPs).

**Called by:** `MLModel.fit`

<sub>src/models/ml.py:34</sub>

### `MLModel`

```python
MLModel(name, features, kind, params=None, note='', refit_every=4)
```

Machine-learning model on the same inputs as the ARDL (lagged growth, drivers, dummies); forecasts are iterated with the macro path like the regressions.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `name` |  | display name. |
| `features` |  | driver features. |
| `kind` |  | see make_estimator. |
| `params` | `None` | estimator settings (may contain refit_every). |
| `note` | `''` | free text. |
| `refit_every` | `4` | quarters between re-estimations in the backtest. |

**Called by:** `build`

<sub>src/models/regime.py:358</sub>

### `MarkovSwitchingARDL`

```python
MarkovSwitchingARDL(name, features, note='', switching_ar=False, switching_variance=False, search_reps=20)
```

Hamilton two-regime Markov-switching ARDL: the intercept (mean growth) switches between a low- and a high-growth regime; the drivers' effects are common (lagged growth may switch too).

Estimated in percent with standardised regressors, which the likelihood search needs with ~100 quarters of small growth rates; coefficients are reported back in the original units.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `name` |  | display name. |
| `features` |  | driver features. |
| `note` | `''` | free text. |
| `switching_ar` | `False` | let the coefficient on lagged growth switch too. |
| `switching_variance` | `False` | give each regime its own volatility (with ~100 quarters this often lets one regime collapse onto a few outliers, so it is off by default). |
| `search_reps` | `20` | random starts in the fit. |

**Called by:** `build`

<sub>src/models/regime.py:372</sub>

### `MarkovSwitchingARDL.__init__`

```python
__init__(name, features, note='', switching_ar=False, switching_variance=False, search_reps=20)
```

| `features` |  | driver features. |
| `note` | `''` | free text. |
| `switching_ar` | `False` | let the coefficient on lagged growth switch too. |
| `switching_variance` | `False` | give each regime its own volatility (with ~100 quarters this often lets one regime collapse onto a few outliers, so it is off by default). |
| `search_reps` | `20` | random starts in the fit. |

**Calls:** `_Regime.__init__`

<sub>src/models/regime.py:604</sub>

### `TimeVaryingARDL`

```python
TimeVaryingARDL(name, features, note='')
```

ARDL whose coefficients follow random walks, estimated with the Kalman filter.

The signal-to-noise ratio q (how fast the coefficients may drift) is chosen by maximum likelihood on a grid; the dummies' coefficients are held fixed. Forecasts use the latest filtered coefficients.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `name` |  | display name. |
| `features` |  | driver features. |
| `note` | `''` | free text. |

**Called by:** `build`

<sub>src/models/regime.py:621</sub>

### `TimeVaryingARDL.__init__`

```python
__init__(name, features, note='')
```

Create a time-varying-parameter model.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `name` |  | display name. |
| `features` |  | driver features. |
| `note` | `''` | free text. |

**Calls:** `_Regime.__init__`

<sub>src/models/regime.py:632</sub>

### `TimeVaryingARDL._kalman`

```python
_kalman(X, y, q, keep_path=False)
```

Kalman filter for y_t = X_t b_t + e_t, b_t = b_t-1 + w_t, with Var(w) = q * s2 * diag(scale).

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `X` |  | regressors (standardised). |
| `y` |  | target. |
| `q` |  | drift ratio. |
| `keep_path` | `False` | return the filtered coefficients. |

**Returns:** (concentrated log-likelihood, last coefficients, path or None, s2).

**Calls:** `BankFeatures.array`

**Called by:** `TimeVaryingARDL.fit`

## src/models/simple.py

Same-growth models: the target grows at the same rate as another variable.

### `SameGrowth`

```python
SameGrowth(name, feature, gap=False, family=FAMILY, note='')
```

Target growth equals the growth of one variable (optionally plus a constant gap). Same interface as ARDL.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `name` |  | display name. |
| `feature` |  | the variable's growth as a feature name, e.g. "FYPD.ION\|dlog\|L0". |
| `gap` | `False` | add the average historical gap (target growth minus the variable's growth) as a constant. |
| `family` | `FAMILY` | model family. |
| `note` | `''` | free text for the model list. |

**Called by:** `same_growth_models`, `rebuild`

| `family` | `FAMILY` | model family. |
| `note` | `''` | free text for the model list. |

<sub>src/models/zoo.py:97</sub>

### `wanted`

```python
wanted(cfg, name=None, family=None, names=())
```

Is a model in models.only? Empty or missing models.only = every model.

Entries match a family name, a model name, or the start of a model name (case does not matter). The AR(1) benchmark is always kept: every comparison and test is made against it.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `cfg` |  | configuration. |
| `name` | `None` | model name. |
| `family` | `None` | model family. |
| `names` | `()` | for a whole family before its models exist: the names its models would get. |

**Returns:** bool.

**Config:** `models`

**Called by:** `run`, `run`, `combine`, `scenarios`, `build`

<sub>src/models/zoo.py:109</sub>

### `same_growth_models`

```python
same_growth_models(cfg, ctx, log=print)
```

The same-growth rules listed in models.same_growth, two per variable (exact and with the average gap).

Each entry is a literature concept (e.g. disposable_income), a series name, or a dict {variable, lag (quarters, default 0), gap: both | yes | no}.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `cfg` |  | configuration. |
| `ctx` |  | Context (literature table, metadata, macro history). |
| `log` | `print` | message function. |

**Returns:** list of (model, how-it-is-specified) pairs.

**Config:** `models`

**Calls:** `resolve`, `SameGrowth`

**Called by:** `build`

## src/panel/estimate.py

Panel estimators. All of them end as one equation per bank, so forecasting is the same for every model:

### `ols`

```python
ols(X, y)
```

Least squares: (coefficients, residuals, (X'X)^-1 or None when singular).

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `X` |  |  |
| `y` |  |  |

**Called by:** `PanelModel._own`, `PanelModel._pooled`, `PanelModel._topdown`, `pooled_fit`

<sub>src/panel/estimate.py:22</sub>

### `driscoll_kraay`

```python
driscoll_kraay(X, e, T, XtXi, lags=4)
```

Driscoll-Kraay covariance: scores summed over banks in each quarter, then Newey-West (Bartlett) over quarters. Robust to heteroskedasticity, autocorrelation and correlation across banks in the same quarter. With one bank it is the Newey-West covariance.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `X` |  | stacked regressors. |
| `e` |  | residuals. |
| `T` |  | quarter of each row (int). |
| `XtXi` |  | (X'X)^-1. |
| `lags` | `4` | Bartlett lags. |

**Returns:** covariance matrix of the coefficients.

**Calls:** `BankFeatures.array`

**Called by:** `PanelModel._own`, `PanelModel._pooled`, `PanelModel._topdown`, `pooled_fit`

```python
stacked(pn, X)
```

|---|---|---|

### `PanelModel`

```python
PanelModel(kind, features, name=None, min_obs=20, dk_lags=4)
```

One panel model: kind, features, and after fit() the per-bank equations.

Attributes after fit: coef[bank] = dict(a, rho, beta (array), delta (array)); se[bank] (separate/partial); pooled_table (pooled/partial/topdown: coefficient, Driscoll-Kraay se, t); weights (partial: shrinkage weight per bank and coefficient); share (topdown: c_i, phi_i); total (topdown: the total's equation).

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `kind` |  |  |
| `features` |  | driver feature names ("code\|transform\|Llag"). |
| `name` | `None` | display name. |
| `min_obs` | `20` |  |
| `dk_lags` | `4` |  |

**Called by:** `build_models`, `quick_error`, `build_node`

### `PanelModel._pooled`

```python
_pooled(pn, m, Xr)
```

Fixed effects: a bank intercept for every bank with enough rows, common rho, beta, dummies.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `pn` |  |  |
| `m` |  |  |
| `Xr` |  |  |

**Calls:** `driscoll_kraay`, `ols`

**Called by:** `PanelModel.fit`

<sub>src/panel/estimate.py:129</sub>

### `PanelModel._shrink`

```python
_shrink(pn, m, Xr)
```

Empirical-Bayes partial pooling of rho and beta towards the pooled values (method of moments).

For each coefficient: tau^2 = max(0, mean_i (b_i - b_pooled)^2 - mean_i se_i^2); bank weight w_i = tau^2 / (tau^2 + se_i^2). The intercept and dummies are then re-estimated for each bank with the shrunk slopes held fixed, so the bank's average level of growth stays its own.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `pn` |  |  |
| `m` |  |  |
| `Xr` |  |  |

**Calls:** `PanelModel._own`, `BankFeatures.array`

**Called by:** `PanelModel.fit`

### `BankFeatures`

```python

```

Candidate features, some of which may differ by bank.

common: DataFrame on pn.dates (macro features, the same for every bank). bank: optional array (banks, dates, k) of bank-specific features named bank_names (e.g. each bank's own mortgage rate change in a system), with their metadata (mnemonic, description, geography, theme, expected_sign) in bank_meta. Correlations and VIFs of bank-specific features use their average across banks.

| argument | default | meaning |

| `bank` | `None` |  |
| `bank_names` | `()` |  |
| `bank_meta` | `None` |  |

## src/pipelines/en_first.py

Approach B - 'Elastic Net first' (the user's approach):
all stationary features -> Elastic Net (lambda set so target_n features are chosen; optional stability
selection) -> group correlated picks, keep one per group -> shared final search.

### `run`

```python
run(cfg, ctx, F, forms)
```

Elastic Net first approach: Elastic Net (glmnet-style) -> stability selection -> group correlated picks -> exhaustive search with the same rules as screen first.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `cfg` |  | configuration. |
| `ctx` |  | Context. |
| `F` |  | all candidate features (DataFrame). |
| `forms` |  | transforms per series. |

**Returns:** dict with the final features, the search table and every intermediate table (written to en_first/).

**Config:** `en_first.group_corr`, `en_first.primary_geography`, `en_first.rep_tolerance`, `en_first.stability`, `en_first.target_n`

**Calls:** `feature_sign`, `parse_feature`, `exhaustive_search`, `cluster`, `representatives`, `elastic_net_select`, `stability_selection`, `log`

**Called by:** `main`

<sub>src/pipelines/en_first.py:14</sub>


## src/pipelines/screen_first.py

Approach A - 'screen first':
candidates -> univariate screen (significant, expected sign) -> best form/lag per variable
-> cluster near-duplicates, keep one per cluster -> shortlist -> shared final search.

### `run`

```python
run(cfg, ctx, F, forms)
```

Screen first approach: univariate screen -> correlation clusters -> shortlist -> exhaustive search.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `cfg` |  | configuration. |
| `ctx` |  | Context. |
| `F` |  | all candidate features. |
| `forms` |  | transforms per series. |

**Returns:** dict with the final features, the search table and every intermediate table (written to screen_first/).

**Config:** `final_model.required_themes`, `screen_first.cluster_corr`, `screen_first.primary_geography`, `screen_first.rep_tolerance`, `screen_first.shortlist_max`

**Calls:** `exhaustive_search`, `cluster`, `representatives`, `screen`, `log`

**Called by:** `main`

<sub>src/pipelines/screen_first.py:12</sub>

### `write_calculator`

```python
write_calculator(path, ctx, model, T, Q, info, best_other=None)
```

The recommended model as a live Excel calculator: one sheet per scenario.

Blue cells are data (history and the scenario's future values); yellow cells are the future values to change; black cells are formulas. Each sheet rebuilds every driver from the raw series, then growth and the balance.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `path` |  | .xlsx to write. |
| `ctx` |  | Context. |
| `model` |  | fitted formula model. |
| `T` |  | terms() table. |
| `Q` |  | quarters ahead. |
| `info` |  | dict of facts for the cover sheet (model, basis, error, window). |
| `best_other` | `None` | optional DataFrame, the most accurate model overall when it has no formula (written as values). |

**Config:** `features`

**Calls:** `lookback`, `parse_feature`, `_excel_feature`, `equation_text`, `n_ylags`, `is_log`

**Called by:** `write`

```python
write(cfg, ctx, models, ranking, basis, log=print)
```

| `ranking` |  | model table with the error columns (out-of-sample summary or backtest comparison). |

**Returns:** dict with the chosen models and file paths, or None when no model qualifies.

## src/reporting/report.py

Write tables (CSV + one Excel workbook per folder), charts and a markdown summary.

### `write_tables`

```python
write_tables(folder, tables)
```

Write each table as CSV and all of them as sheets of tables.xlsx (with sized columns).

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `folder` |  | output folder. |
| `tables` |  | dict name -> DataFrame. |

**Called by:** `run`, `run`

<sub>src/reporting/report.py:23</sub>

### `rank_column`

```python
rank_column(comp, H)
```

The error column to rank by: avg_1_<H>_pct, or the longest available horizon up to H (then any).

With few forecast origins the longest horizon that has errors can fall between the report horizons, so the exact column may not exist.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `comp` |  | table with avg_1_<h>_pct columns. |
| `H` |  | preferred horizon. |

**Called by:** `best_per_family`, `scenario_models`, `_rank`

<sub>src/reporting/report.py:50</sub>

### `best_per_family`

```python
best_per_family(comp, H)
```

AR(1) plus the most accurate model of every other family (for charts that cannot show 20 lines).

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `comp` |  | model comparison table. |
| `H` |  | forecast horizon in quarters. |

**Calls:** `rank_column`

**Called by:** `run`, `run`, `chart_oos`

<sub>src/reporting/report.py:69</sub>

### `scenario_models`

```python
scenario_models(comp, H)
```

Models shown in the scenario chart: the data-driven ones and the best literature and hybrid models.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `comp` |  | model comparison table. |
| `H` |  | forecast horizon in quarters. |

**Calls:** `rank_column`

**Called by:** `run`

<sub>src/reporting/report.py:80</sub>

### `chart_horizon`

```python
chart_horizon(path, by_h, models=None)
```

Line chart of balance error by quarters ahead, one line per model, coloured by family.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `path` |  | PNG to write. |
| `by_h` |  | backtest errors by horizon. |
| `models` | `None` | subset to plot (None = all). |

**Called by:** `run`

<sub>src/reporting/report.py:94</sub>

### `chart_family_bars`

```python
chart_family_bars(path, comp, horizons)
```

Average balance error over 1..h quarters for every model, grouped by family; one panel per horizon.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `path` |  | file to write or read. |

| `horizons` |  | comparison horizons, e.g. [4, 8, 12]. |

**Called by:** `run`, `run`

## src/reporting/story.py

The data story: how the data moves through the model, one step at a time, with this run's own numbers.

### `_rank`

```python
_rank(t, H)
```

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `t` |  |  |
| `H` |  | forecast horizon in quarters. |

**Calls:** `rank_column`

**Called by:** `build_oos`

<sub>src/reporting/story.py:19</sub>

### `fmt`

```python
fmt(v, d=4)
```

Short display of a value for a story table.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `v` |  | value. |
| `d` | `4` | significant digits for floats. |

**Returns:** str, number or None.

**Called by:** `step`, `tbl`

<sub>src/reporting/story.py:31</sub>

### `tbl`

```python
tbl(df, label, rows=10, note='', highlight=None)
```

A table for the story: first rows, values shortened.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `df` |  | DataFrame. |
| `label` |  | caption. |
| `rows` | `10` | rows kept. |
| `note` | `''` | one line under it. |
| `highlight` | `None` | column and value whose rows are marked (the followed variable), e.g. ("feature", "X\|dlog\|L1"). |

**Returns:** dict (label, columns, rows, n, note, marked).

**Calls:** `fmt`

**Called by:** `_model_steps`, `build`, `build_oos`

<sub>src/reporting/story.py:53</sub>

### `step`

```python
step(stage, title, text, equation=None, rules=None, numbers=None, tables=None, follow=None, functions=None, files=None)
```

One step of the story.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `stage` |  | short stage label. |
| `title` |  | the step's finding. |
| `text` |  | list of short paragraphs. |
| `equation` | `None` | lines of the equation(s). |
| `rules` | `None` | the decision rules. |
| `numbers` | `None` | list of (label, value). |
| `tables` | `None` | list from tbl(). |
| `follow` | `None` | what happens to the followed variable here. |
| `functions` | `None` | functions where it happens (keys of the call tree). |
| `files` | `None` | output files with the full tables. |

**Returns:** dict.

**Calls:** `fmt`

**Called by:** `_model_steps`, `build`, `build_oos`

<sub>src/reporting/story.py:71</sub>

### `_pick_follow`

```python
_pick_follow(cfg, results)
```

The feature followed through the story: config trace.follow, else the first driver chosen by the first approach.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `cfg` |  | configuration. |
| `results` |  | approach results. |

**Returns:** feature name.

**Config:** `trace`

**Called by:** `build`

<sub>src/reporting/story.py:89</sub>

### `build`

```python
build(cfg, ctx, forms, F, results, models, notes, ev, comp)
```

The story of one pipeline run.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `cfg` |  | configuration. |
| `ctx` |  | Context. |
| `forms` |  | transforms and ADF results. |
| `F` |  | candidate features. |
| `results` |  | approach results. |
| `models` |  | the model zoo. |
| `notes` |  | model list. |
| `ev` |  | evaluation per model. |
| `comp` |  | model comparison table. |

**Returns:** dict with the followed variable and the list of steps.

**Config:** `data.bank.target_column`, `en_first.group_corr`, `en_first.l1_ratio`, `en_first.stability`, `en_first.target_n`, `features`, `final_model.max_vars`, `final_model.min_vars`, `final_model.one_per_theme`, `final_model.selection_tolerance`, `final_model.t_min`, `final_model.vif_max`, `sample`, `screen_first.cluster_corr`, `screen_first.primary_geography`, `screen_first.rep_tolerance`, `screen_first.screen_p`

**Calls:** `feature_matrix`, `parse_feature`, `tr_desc`, `transform`, `_model_steps`, `_pick_follow`, `step`, `tbl`, `growth`, `is_log`, `level_after`, `per_year`

**Called by:** `run`

<sub>src/reporting/story.py:108</sub>

### `_model_steps`

```python
_model_steps(cfg, ctx, m, ev, fol)
```

Steps 11-14 for the chosen model: estimation, one backtest origin worked through, the forecast taken apart.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `cfg` |  | configuration. |
| `ctx` |  | Context. |
| `m` |  | the model. |
| `ev` |  | evaluation per model. |
| `fol` |  | followed feature. |

**Returns:** list of steps.

**Config:** `sample.backtest_end`, `sample.backtest_start`, `sample.estimation_start`, `sample.horizon`, `sample.reestimate_every`

## src/selection/clustering.py

Group features whose |correlation| exceeds a threshold and keep one representative per group.

### `cluster`

```python
cluster(F, corr_threshold)
```

Average-linkage clustering on distance 1 - |corr|. Returns a Series of cluster labels (one per feature).

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `F` |  | candidate features (DataFrame, one column per feature). |
| `corr_threshold` |  | \|correlation\| above which series join the same cluster. |

**Called by:** `shortlist`, `run`, `run`, `main`

<sub>src/selection/clustering.py:8</sub>

### `representatives`

```python
representatives(table, score_col, primary_geography=None, tolerance=0.75)
```

Within each cluster keep the highest score, unless a primary-geography member scores at least `tolerance` x the best, in which case keep the best primary-geography member.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `table` |  | one row per variable with a cluster column. |
| `score_col` |  | column to rank by. |
| `primary_geography` | `None` | preferred geography. |
| `tolerance` | `0.75` | share of the best score a primary-geography member needs. |

**Called by:** `shortlist`, `run`, `run`, `main`

<sub>src/selection/clustering.py:22</sub>


## src/selection/elastic_net.py

Elastic Net selection (the 'EN first' approach), matching glmnet's conventions.

### `_prepare`

```python
_prepare(cfg, ctx, F, rows)
```

Standardise the candidates and, if unpenalized_controls, partial out lagged growth and dummies (Frisch-Waugh) so the Elastic Net only shrinks the macro drivers.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `cfg` |  | configuration. |
| `ctx` |  | Context. |
| `F` |  | candidate features. |
| `rows` |  | positions used. |

**Returns:** (X standardised, y, feature names, expected signs).

**Config:** `en_first.sign_constraints`, `en_first.unpenalized_controls`

**Calls:** `feature_sign`, `BankFeatures.array`, `controls`

**Called by:** `elastic_net_select`, `stability_selection`, `main`

<sub>src/selection/elastic_net.py:21</sub>

### `_select_at_target`

```python
_select_at_target(cfg, X, y, names, signs)
```

Walk down a log-spaced lambda grid with warm starts and stop at the first lambda where target_n features are non-zero (same answer as reading it off the full path, much faster).

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `cfg` |  | the run configuration (from load_config). |
| `X` |  | standardised candidates. |
| `y` |  | target (controls partialled out). |
| `names` |  | feature names. |
| `signs` |  | expected signs. |

**Config:** `en_first.l1_ratio`, `en_first.sign_constraints`, `en_first.target_n`

**Calls:** `BankFeatures.array`

**Called by:** `elastic_net_select`, `stability_selection`

<sub>src/selection/elastic_net.py:58</sub>

### `elastic_net_select`

```python
elastic_net_select(cfg, ctx, F)
```

Elastic Net on the full estimation sample, with lambda set so that target_n features are kept.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `cfg` |  | configuration. |
| `ctx` |  | Context. |
| `F` |  | candidate features. |

**Returns:** DataFrame: feature, standardised coefficient, lambda.

**Calls:** `_prepare`, `_select_at_target`

**Called by:** `run`, `main`

<sub>src/selection/elastic_net.py:86</sub>

### `stability_selection`

```python
stability_selection(cfg, ctx, F)
```

Re-run the Elastic Net on many block subsamples and count how often each feature is picked.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `cfg` |  | configuration (en_first.stability). |
| `ctx` |  | Context. |
| `F` |  | candidate features. |

**Returns:** DataFrame: feature, selection_frequency (sorted).

**Config:** `en_first.stability.block_length`, `en_first.stability.fraction`, `en_first.stability.n_subsamples`, `en_first.stability.seed`

**Calls:** `_prepare`, `_select_at_target`

**Called by:** `run`, `main`

<sub>src/selection/elastic_net.py:103</sub>


## src/selection/screening.py

Univariate screen: each candidate is added on its own to a base regression with lagged target growth
and the dummies, using Newey-West (HAC) standard errors.

### `controls`

```python
controls(ctx)
```

Control variables in every screening regression: constant, lagged growth and COVID dummies.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `ctx` |  | Context. |

**Returns:** DataFrame on ctx.idx.

**Called by:** `_prepare`, `screen`, `main`

<sub>src/selection/screening.py:13</sub>

### `screen`

```python
screen(cfg, ctx, F)
```

Univariate screen: regress growth on the controls plus one feature at a time (Newey-West t).

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `cfg` |  | configuration (screen_first). |
| `ctx` |  | Context. |
| `F` |  | candidate features. |

**Returns:** (all results, best form per variable with a passed flag: p-value and expected sign).

**Config:** `screen_first.screen_p`

**Calls:** `feature_sign`, `parse_feature`, `controls`

**Called by:** `_screen_tables`, `run`, `main`

<sub>src/selection/screening.py:26</sub>

<sub>src/system/run.py:340</sub>

### `BVARX`

```python
BVARX(names, forms, exog, lam=0.2)
```

VAR(1) of every target's change with exogenous macro drivers; cross-target lags shrunk towards 0.

Equation i: y_i,t = c_i + sum_j a_ij y_j,t-1 + b_i' x_t + e_i,t. Prior: a_ij ~ N(0, (lam * s_i / s_j)^2) for j != i (s = residual std. dev. of a univariate AR(1)); own lag, constant and macro effects flat. The posterior mean is a ridge regression with those penalties.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `names` |  |  |
| `forms` |  | chosen transforms per series. |
| `exog` |  |  |
| `lam` | `0.2` |  |

**Called by:** `bvarx_backtest`

<sub>src/system/run.py:650</sub>

### `reconcile_all`

```python
reconcile_all(scfg, base_cfg, data, members, outs, E, paths, out_dir, H, log)
```

For each identity (total = sum of parts): forecast the total on its own (single-target project, macro drivers only), then reconcile the chain's part forecasts with it, horizon by horizon, in the backtest and in every scenario.

| `E` |  |  |
| `paths` |  |  |
| `out_dir` |  |  |
| `H` |  | forecast horizon in quarters. |
| `log` |  | function used to print progress messages. |

**Config:** `data.macro.scenarios`, `hyperparameters`, `identities`, `presets`

**Calls:** `BankFeatures.array`, `members_by`, `reconcile_levels`

## src/utils/config.py

Load the YAML configuration, fill defaults, resolve paths and check that the required names are there.

### `_fix_switches`

```python
_fix_switches(cfg, base=None, path=())
```

Read true/false/yes/no written where a list or a block is expected the way it is meant.

YAML turns the words true, yes, on (and ON, the Ontario code) into True, and false, no, off into False. A switch written in place of a list or block (e.g. statistical: yes, deep: false) would otherwise stop the run with "'bool' object is not iterable". True keeps the default list or block (switched on), False switches it off.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `cfg` |  | merged configuration (changed in place). |
| `base` | `None` | the defaults at the same level. |
| `path` | `()` | keys so far. |

**Returns:** list of "path: value -> meaning" notes, for the log.

**Called by:** `load_config`

<sub>src/utils/config.py:80</sub>

### `_merge`

```python
_merge(base, over)
```

Recursively merge a user config over the defaults (user values win).

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `base` |  | defaults. |
| `over` |  | user values. |

**Returns:** new dict.

**Called by:** `load_config`

<sub>src/utils/config.py:114</sub>

### `_path`

```python
_path(p)
```

Resolve a path relative to the project folder.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `p` |  | path or None. |

**Returns:** absolute path or None.

**Called by:** `load_config`

<sub>src/utils/config.py:129</sub>

### `load_config`

```python
load_config(path)
```

Read a YAML config, fill defaults, check required keys and normalise values.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `path` |  | config file. |

**Returns:** dict (also holds out_dir and config_file).

**Config:** `cache.dir`, `config_file`, `data.bank`, `data.events`, `data.macro`, `data.metadata.file`, `literature.master`, `literature.models`, `literature.table`, `literature.target`, `literature.workbook`, `oos.first_origin`, `oos.window`, `out_dir`, `output_dir`, `run_name`, `sample.backtest_end`, `sample.backtest_start`, `sample.dummy_quarters`, `sample.horizon`, `sample.reestimate_quarter`, `sample.report_horizons`

**Calls:** `apply_settings`, `read_workbook`, `_fix_switches`, `_merge`, `_path`, `apply`

**Called by:** `main`, `main`, `_target_cfg`, `Member.__init__`, `main`, `main`, `build_deck`, `main`, `make`, `main`

### `parse_docstring`

```python
parse_docstring(doc, params=())
```

Split a Google-style docstring into summary, description and sections.

Arguments may be written one per line or several on a line ("P: panel. start, end: window."); only names that are real parameters start a new argument.

|---|---|---|
| `doc` |  | docstring text (or None). |
| `params` | `()` | the function's parameter names. |

**Returns:** dict with summary, body (list of paragraphs), args (name -> text), returns, raises, other sections.

## src/utils/saved.py

Keep the results of every run so they can be opened later without running anything again.

### `save_fitted`

```python
save_fitted(out_dir, fitted, log=print)
```

Write the fitted models of a run to fitted/models.pkl.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `out_dir` |  | the run's output folder. |
| `fitted` |  | dict model name -> pickle bytes (None = not stored). |
| `log` | `print` | message function. |

**Returns:** path of the file, or None if nothing could be stored.

**Calls:** `portable`

**Called by:** `save_all`

<sub>src/utils/saved.py:32</sub>

### `_slug`

```python
_slug(name)
```

A model name as a safe file name.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `name` |  | display name. |

**Called by:** `load_origin_models`, `save_all`

<sub>src/utils/saved.py:86</sub>

### `save_all`

```python
save_all(out_dir, ev, ctx, log=print)
```

Everything estimated in a run, next to models.pkl (fitted/):

models.pkl                    every model estimated on all history (load_models()) formula_models.json           regressions as plain numbers (any computer, any package version) coefficients.csv              every coefficient: estimate, standard error, t, p value (regressions), or weights / parameters for the other model types fit_statistics.csv            observations, R², adjusted R², AIC, BIC, residual std. dev. per model fitted_values.csv             in-sample one-step fit of every model: actual, fitted, residual, levels panels/<model>.csv            the data each model was estimated on (history and baseline scenario) backtest_models/<model>.pkl   the model as re-estimated at every backtest origin {origin: model} (load_origin_models()) data/target.csv               the target: level, organic change in the model's form, cumulated level manifest.json                 what was saved, the target form and the file of each model

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `out_dir` |  | the run's output folder. |
| `ev` |  | model name -> evaluate() output. |
| `ctx` |  | Context. |
| `log` | `print` | message function. |

**Returns:** the fitted/ folder.

**Calls:** `_slug`, `save_fitted`, `form`, `units`

**Called by:** `run`

<sub>src/utils/saved.py:92</sub>

```python
save_origin_models(folder, store, log=print)
```

Out-of-sample models: {origin: {model name: pickle bytes}} -> folder/<origin>.pkl, one file per origin.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `folder` |  | output folder. |
| `store` |  |  |
| `log` | `print` | function used to print progress messages. |

**Called by:** `run`

| `model` | `None` | a model following the model interface. |

**Returns:** dict origin (or model name) -> fitted model.

```python
snapshot(cfg, script, log=print)
```

Copy the run's current results into saved_runs/<date_time>_<script>/ and keep the newest save.keep_runs.

## src/utils/trace.py

Detailed run trace: records every call to a function in the project while the pipeline runs.

### `_cell`

```python
_cell(v)
```

Make a table cell JSON-safe (rounded floats, NaN -> None, numpy scalars -> Python).

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `v` |  | value. |

**Returns:** JSON-serialisable value.

**Called by:** `compact`, `summarize`, `table_json`

<sub>src/utils/trace.py:68</sub>

### `_is_fit_result`

```python
_is_fit_result(v)
```

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `v` |  | any object. |

**Returns:** bool.

**Called by:** `Tracer._locals`, `summarize`, `tables_in`

<sub>src/utils/trace.py:90</sub>

### `summarize`

```python
summarize(v)
```

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `v` |  | any value. |

| `v` |  | any value. |

```python
_project_function(x)
```

A plain function or lambda written in this project (not a library, not this file).

|---|---|---|
| `x` |  | any object. |

<sub>src/utils/trace.py:254</sub>

### `Tracer`

```python
Tracer(folder, title, preview_rows=20, save_tables=True, detail_calls=1, distinct_labels=8, max_table_cells=200000, local_variables=True, local_preview_rows=8, max_repeats_listed=500)
```

Records every call of the project's functions (arguments, results, local variables, logs, time) and writes trace.json, trace.log and trace.html.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `folder` |  | output folder. |
| `title` |  | page title. |
| `preview_rows` | `20` | table rows shown. |
| `save_tables` | `True` | save returned tables as CSV. |
| `detail_calls` | `1` | repeated calls recorded in full. |
| `distinct_labels` | `8` | calls with different text arguments shown separately. |
| `max_table_cells` | `200000` | larger tables are not saved. |
| `local_variables` | `True` | record local variables. |
| `local_preview_rows` | `8` | rows per local table. |
| `max_repeats_listed` | `500` | repeated calls listed per node. |

**Called by:** `start`

<sub>src/utils/trace.py:267</sub>

### `Tracer.__init__`

```python
__init__(folder, title, preview_rows=20, save_tables=True, detail_calls=1, distinct_labels=8, max_table_cells=200000, local_variables=True, local_preview_rows=8, max_repeats_listed=500)
```

Create a tracer writing to folder; the settings come from the trace: section of the config.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `folder` |  | output folder. |
| `title` |  | page title. |
| `preview_rows` | `20` | table rows shown. |
| `save_tables` | `True` | save returned tables as CSV. |
| `detail_calls` | `1` | repeated calls recorded in full. |
| `distinct_labels` | `8` | calls with different text arguments shown separately. |
| `max_table_cells` | `200000` | larger tables are not saved. |
| `local_variables` | `True` | record local variables. |
| `local_preview_rows` | `8` | rows per local table. |
| `max_repeats_listed` | `500` | repeated calls listed per node. |

<sub>src/utils/trace.py:271</sub>

### `Tracer._register`

```python
_register(fn, library=False, display=None)
```

Add a function to the trace's function list (file, line, docstring, source).

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `fn` |  | function. |
| `library` | `False` | True for a library routine. |
| `display` | `None` | name to show. |

**Returns:** the function's key.

**Called by:** `Tracer.wrap`

<sub>src/utils/trace.py:304</sub>

### `Tracer.wrap`

```python
wrap(fn, library=False, display=None, cache=True)
```

Wrap a function so each call is recorded as a node of the call tree.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `fn` |  | function. |
| `library` | `False` | library routine. |
| `display` | `None` | name to show. |
| `cache` | `True` | reuse an existing wrapper. |

**Returns:** the wrapper.

**Calls:** `Tracer._example_call`, `Tracer._register`, `Tracer._safe`, `_project_function`, `compact`

**Called by:** `Tracer.install`

<sub>src/utils/trace.py:334</sub>

### `Tracer._safe`

```python
_safe(f, *a)
```

Call f, returning None instead of raising (tracing must never break a run).

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `f` |  | function. |
| `*a` |  | its arguments. |

**Called by:** `Tracer._example_call`, `Tracer.wrap`

<sub>src/utils/trace.py:450</sub>

### `Tracer._args`

```python
_args(sig, a, k)
```

Summaries of a call's arguments (name, type, size, detail) and their table previews.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `sig` |  | inspect.Signature or None. |
| `a` |  | positional and keyword arguments. |
| `k` |  | positional and keyword arguments. |

**Returns:** list of {name, type, size, detail}.

**Calls:** `summarize`

<sub>src/utils/trace.py:461</sub>

### `Tracer._example_call`

```python
_example_call(key, sig, fn, a, k)
```

Run a call inside a folded (repeated) call and keep its arguments and result as the function's example.

Only the first such call of each function is kept, so functions that only ever run inside repeated calls (e.g. evaluate inside cached steps) still get an example in the function reference.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `key` |  | function key. |
| `sig` |  | its signature. |
| `fn` |  | the original function. |
| `a` |  | the call's arguments. |
| `k` |  | the call's arguments. |

**Returns:** the function's return value.

**Calls:** `Tracer._safe`

**Called by:** `Tracer.wrap`

<sub>src/utils/trace.py:487</sub>

### `Tracer._locals`

```python
_locals(node, frame)
```

Variables in the function's frame when it returned (or failed).

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `node` |  | call node. |
| `frame` |  | the function's frame. |

**Calls:** `_is_fit_result`, `coef_table`, `summarize`, `table_json`

<sub>src/utils/trace.py:515</sub>

### `Tracer._result`

```python
_result(node, r)
```

Store a summary and table previews of a call's return value on its node.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `node` |  | call node. |
| `r` |  | return value. |

**Calls:** `summarize`, `table_json`, `tables_in`

<sub>src/utils/trace.py:544</sub>

```python
log(msg)
```

Attach a progress message to the call currently running.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `msg` |  | text. |

## src/validation/oos.py

True out-of-sample tests for every model family.

### `inner_window`

```python
inner_window(cfg, ctx, end_pos)
```

The backtest window used for selection at an origin. The configured window (sample.backtest_start to backtest_end) is kept if the origin is past it; otherwise a window of the same length ending at the origin is used (it must start at least 5 years after estimation_start), so early origins can still rank drivers.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `cfg` |  | the run configuration (from load_config). |
| `ctx` |  | the Context: loaded data, organic growth, scenarios, metadata and the estimation window. |
| `end_pos` |  | position of the last quarter the step may use. |

**Config:** `sample.backtest_end`, `sample.backtest_start`, `sample.estimation_start`

**Called by:** `truncated`

<sub>src/validation/oos.py:41</sub>

### `truncated`

```python
truncated(ctx, end_pos, cfg)
```

A copy of the context that only 'knows' data up to end_pos (and, for a rolling window, from start).

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `ctx` |  | the Context: loaded data, organic growth, scenarios, metadata and the estimation window. |
| `end_pos` |  | position of the last quarter the step may use. |
| `cfg` |  | the run configuration (from load_config). |

**Config:** `oos.window`, `oos.window_length`

**Calls:** `inner_window`

**Called by:** `models_at`, `select_at`

<sub>src/validation/oos.py:59</sub>

| `end_pos` |  | position of the last quarter the step may use. |

| `approach` |  | selection approach: screen_first or en_first. |

### `forecast_errors`

```python
forecast_errors(ctx, model, origin_pos, H, est_start, detail=False)
```

Fit on est_start..origin and forecast H quarters; return level % errors (organic balance).

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `ctx` |  | the Context: loaded data, organic growth, scenarios, metadata and the estimation window. |
| `model` |  | a model following the model interface. |
| `origin_pos` |  | position of the forecast origin. |

**Returns:** errors (fractions), or (errors, forecast growth, actual growth) with detail=True.

**Called by:** `run_oos`

<sub>src/validation/oos.py:115</sub>

### `dm_test`

```python
dm_test(e_model, e_bench, h)
```

Diebold-Mariano on squared level errors, HAC variance with h-1 lags, HLN correction. Negative = model better.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `e_model` |  | model errors. |
| `e_bench` |  | benchmark errors. |
| `h` |  | horizon. |

**Called by:** `summarise`

<sub>src/validation/oos.py:133</sub>

### `run_oos`

```python
run_oos(cfg, ctx, approaches, full_sample_drivers, cache, store=None)
```

Out-of-sample test: at each origin, re-run selection and re-estimate every model with data up to the origin only, then forecast 1..H quarters.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `cfg` |  | configuration (oos). |
| `ctx` |  | Context. |
| `approaches` |  | name -> pipeline module. |
| `full_sample_drivers` |  | drivers chosen with all data (for the look-ahead check). |
| `cache` |  | StepCache. |
| `store` | `None` | optional dict, filled with {origin: {model name: pickle bytes}} (each model as estimated at that origin) and store["__coefficients__"]: list of coefficient rows by origin. |

**Returns:** (errors by model/origin/horizon, drivers picked at each origin, horizon).

**Config:** `oos.first_origin`, `oos.horizon`, `oos.step`, `oos.window`, `sample.dummy_quarters`, `sample.estimation_start`, `sample.horizon`

**Calls:** `freeze`, `log`, `growth_display`, `level_after`, `forecast_errors`, `models_at`

**Called by:** `run`, `main`

<sub>src/validation/oos.py:155</sub>

### `summarise`

```python
summarise(E, picks, H, horizons)
```

Summarise out-of-sample errors: MAPE, RMSE, bias and Theil's U by horizon, and the averages over 1..h for each comparison horizon.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `E` |  | errors from run_oos. |
| `picks` |  | drivers by origin. |
| `H` |  | horizon. |
| `horizons` |  | comparison horizons. |

**Returns:** (summary per model, errors by horizon).

**Calls:** `dm_test`

**Called by:** `run`, `main`

<sub>src/validation/oos.py:219</sub>

### `holdout`

```python
holdout(cfg, ctx, approaches, cache)
```

Select and estimate every model on data up to hist_end - holdout_quarters; forecast the held-out quarters.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `cfg` |  | the run configuration (from load_config). |
| `ctx` |  | the Context: loaded data, organic growth, scenarios, metadata and the estimation window. |
| `approaches` |  | selection approaches to run. |
| `cache` |  | the StepCache. |

**Config:** `oos.holdout_quarters`, `oos.window`, `sample.estimation_start`

**Calls:** `log`, `error`, `level_after`, `models_at`

**Called by:** `run`

<sub>src/validation/oos.py:279</sub>

## tools/process_story.py

The process story of a run, for an audience: what happened at every step, with the run's own numbers and charts.

### `make`

```python
make(config, run_dir=None, fmt='both', out_dir=None, log=print)
```

Build the story of a finished run.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `config` |  | config file. |
| `run_dir` | `None` | the run's output folder (default outputs/<run_name>). |
| `fmt` | `'both'` | html, pptx or both. |
| `out_dir` | `None` | where to write (default <run_dir>/process_story). |
| `log` | `print` | function used to print progress messages. |

**Returns:** dict with the paths written and any section that could not be built.

**Config:** `out_dir`, `run_name`

**Calls:** `Story`, `Story.build`, `build`, `build`, `load_config`

**Called by:** `main`

<sub>report/style_deck.py:599</sub>

### `fix_empty_text`

```python
fix_empty_text(prs)
```

Make every text box valid for PowerPoint: a text body must hold at least one paragraph.

When a slide's content is left out (e.g. no out-of-sample results) the design markers are removed and a placeholder can end up with an empty <p:txBody>, which PowerPoint "repairs" by dropping shapes. Empty placeholders are removed; any other empty text body gets an empty paragraph.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `prs` |  | Presentation (changed in place). |

**Returns:** number of shapes fixed.

**Called by:** `main`

<sub>report/style_deck.py:608</sub>

### `check_package`

```python
check_package(path)
```

Look for the faults PowerPoint refuses (it then asks to repair the file and drops content).

Checks every slide and chart: text bodies without a paragraph, duplicate shape ids on a slide, relationships to missing parts, and 'nan' / 'inf' numbers in chart data.

| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `path` |  | .pptx file. |

**Returns:** list of problem descriptions (empty = clean).

**Calls:** `search`, `Story.read`

**Called by:** `main`

<sub>report/style_deck.py:637</sub>


| argument | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `prs` |  | python-pptx Presentation. |

**Called by:** `main`
